Hayden Trantham NDIS 431194185

e Being charged the minimum 6 hours per day for Support workers as per SIL contract

e The amount of Support work provided by FFG/The Hive is irregular and rarely meets the
required hours

e Hayden has complained a few times about not having Support workers attending regularly

e Support workers are provided adhoc and are not required to have mental health experience
even though Hayden has a psychosocial diagnosis

e Hayden was hospitalised a couple of months ago due to a suicide attempt.

e Even when Hayden was discharged, the Support Workers provided by the Hive were
irregular.

e Hayden once again complained about not having supports

e Hayden sent several text messages to his Psychosocial recovery coach, that indicated suicidal
intent. She arranged for him to be hospitalised, where he has been for the last month, being
reassessed and receiving treatment.

e Hayden is being financially abused, by regularly being charged for supports he is not
receiving. He is being physically, emotionally and mentally abused by not receiving the
qualified supports he avn to the point that he felt so yore s he rene suicidal.

another > pankicrpeear's fot hor é PAY S ay ~ AO Eexpevren ce
or QUAN CPF, ch NS

Meghan Goodwin NDIS 431576199 Ahad Andrew Hahn NDIS

Meghan and Andrew were both residents of a property in Safety Bay, provided by Forward Focused
Group/The Hive. While they wouldn’t have been considered friends, they had a respectful
relationship as housemates, conversing at times, but also having their private space. They were both
being charged for 8 hours of Support Work per day, but only received a cook for approx. 9 hours per
week and a cleaner for the same. Andrew began to ask questions about the amount he was being
charged, while not receiving the corresponding supports. Andrew has an advocate who assisted him
to question the validity of the charges being invoiced, and when no answers were forthcoming from
the company, Andrew refused to pay any further invoices related to supports. Vida Reid (Director)
became abusive toward Andrew. She arranged for letters from her lawyers to threaten him with
eviction, which she did eventually do, Andrew owned a couple of Japanese Samurai swords which he
used for decoration, but Vida told Meghan that Andrew was dangerous and had threatened to use
the swords to harm her. Meghan didn’t know what to believe so sometimes she wouldn’t go home
because she was afraid and would sleep on the trains or spend the night at the gym. At one point
Vida arranged STA for Meghan at a loca! motel, for approximately a week, but only allowed her to
sleep there, and made no provision for food. Obviously this was charged from her plan, but she
didn’t receive the food or support work that should have been included as part of STA.

On Tuesday 12° April, Vida Reid arranged for one of the male Support Workers employed by The
Hive, to do a night shift as “security” in the house, under the guise of providing “protection” for
Meghan from Andrew. The Support Worker is quite a large, imposing man and Vida joked that this
would “set Andy off”. During the night of Wednesday 13" April, Meghan was in the kitchen and the
Support Worker came out of his bedroom naked, which naturally freaked Meghan out. The next
morning, Meghan reported this to her Support Coordinator when he arrived at work, and she was
still agitated and distressed. The Support Coordinator rang and told Vida Reid, who could be heard
laughing as the incident was relayed to her. She then went into the office and described the incident
to her husband Simon Reid, arid the Chief Financial Officer, Rejaul Alam, and once again the
response was one of hilarity. Vida made the comment “we put him in there to scare Andy and its

245

=== PDF page 256 (T9.14) [OCR] ===
T9.14

instead done that to Meghan”. Vida came out and shared the “hilarious” story with the rest of the
staff in the office — some staff laughed, others were quiet, and i suggested that Meghan might
actually choose to report it to the police. Clearly this is totally inappropriate behaviour by the
Support Worker, but apart from pulling him out of the house, there was no other action taken by
FFG or The Hive around this incident. In fact, the support worker is still working for the company in
the role of support worker as well as other office roles. It also highlights the lengths that they are
prepared to go to in setting Meghan and Andrew against each other and causing them to be
suspicious of each other’s behaviour and intentions. The whole situation is detrimental to the
mental health of both of them, particularly Meghan, who is scared to go home at night and finds the
option of staying out for the whole night a safer option.

Even though Andrew had no other accommodation, Vida gave him an eviction notice and changed
the locks on the house so he couldn’t get back in.

Neither Meghan or Andrew had SIL approved in their NDIS plan, so their plans were constantly
having to be renewed due to being overcharged for supports. Vida has regularly submitted a Change
of Circumstance when Meghan’s funds run low and makes up different reasons for requiring extra
funds. For example, she once rang NDIA and said Meghan required emergency funding because she
was such a compiex case. She said that Meghan was so unstable that she had had a meltdown and
put her head through a wall. This had not actually happened, but was similar to an incident that had
happened a few years earlier while Meghan was in different accommodation and was using drugs.
Meghan has had 6 or 7 plans within less than a 12 month period and her current plan has had over
$40,000 used within a 2 month period. Meghan is now aware of this and is questioning Vida's
motives. Vida is constantly reminding her that she would have nothing of it wasn’t for Vida. For
example, she told Meghan that she had personally purchased her a set of Apple Noise Cancelling
headphones, however the Support Coordinator arranged for the purchase and they were paid for
from Meghan’s Plan. There is a letter of support from her psychologist and the invoices, etc to prove
it. Vida attempts to control Meghan via her personal finances — she has called an ambulance so
many times when it has not been necessary and Meghan now has a debt of thousands of dollars
with St John’s Ambulance that she is paying off out of her pension, to the point that she barely has
any remaining funds. Vida pays for her phone, but uses it as a form of control. Meghan feels
“indebted” to Vida and is trapped by this.

There are multiple other examples of financial, emotional, mental and physical abuse to participants.
Some of them are aware and have removed Forward Focused Group and The Hive as their provider.
Many others, though, have an intellectual disability and are unaware that they are being defrauded
out of their NDIS funds, however, the fact that they don’t know, does not make it any more
acceptable, They are still being financially abused.

Subject: FW: Case number 1A0000031700RE

To whom it may concern,

| have previously lodged information for the case number above and would like to add
further information regarding invoices from Forward Focused Group (FFG) and Hybrid
Hive (The Hive) that are being raised for services that were not provided.

On Tuesday the 12" April, | overheard the Director of FFG, Vida Reid, on the phone
commenting that a participant had received a new plan (Bassarn Haidar, NDIS
431524488, DOB 24/12/1956). Within minutes, Vida approached the two new Accounts
Officers (one for FFG and the other for The Hive) and directed them to open the
Provider Choice portal to see the remaining balance of funds from the old plan. Based
on the amount remaining they were instructed to raise invoices for a two-week period
for counselling (FFG) and also cleaning and Support Work (The Hive} - None of these
services even occurred. These were completed and sent, and | attach them for your
perusal. You will see that most of The Hive invoices for Support Work were all raised on
13/04/2022. These amount to $53,354.56. All of the invoices total $61,453.23.
Coincidentally, there is $74,967.12 remaining in the old plan.

Hayden Trantham (NDIS 431194185, DOB 03/11/1986) moved into a SIL property in
early April. He has only been provided with occasional Support Workers since moving in
but has still been invoiced for the 6 hours a day he was promised (see attached). Also,
with regard to the names listed on the invoice - Honey is his Support Coordinator, not a
Support Worker, and David is not even a member of The Hive staff. David had provided
Hayden with support on one day but that had already been accurately invoiced
previously and David has not provided support work since then as David is employed by
another provider and lives in the southwest (where Hayden previously resided before
moving to Rockingham).

The new Accounts Officer for FFG was also directed to raise invoices for Sarah_Jane
Clulow (NDIS 430838840, DOB 16/05/1987) for counselling and STA. | have attached

these for you. You will see that for invoice INV-0983, counselling is invoiced for 3.5 to 4

invoiced, | don’t know how she would feel about her name being used for a fraudulent
invoice. Vida Reid had also directed the Accounts Manager, Danica Hall (who has also
been emailing you about all this), to also do an invoice for Support Work for Sarah-Jane
for up to $60,000.00, after Vida noticed that the remaining balance in Sarah-Jane’s old

(however, this Support Work was never provided by FFG). It’s possible that Vida may

1. INV-0895 - Support Work for 14/03/2022 to 20/03/2022 for 6 hours each day.

3. INV-0942 - Support Work for 28/03/2022 to 03/04/2022 for 6 hours each day.

from the Hive’s Xero.

For another participant, Daniel Clark (NDIS 430901549, DOB 02/06/1992), it came up in
conversation with Vida that | was doing his SIL application. Knowing that | was
requesting 1:1 supports, Vida suggested that | could do it as a 2:1. { said that he didn’t
need 2:1, he only requires 1:1 and Vida’s response was “have you met him?” |
responded saying that | had but that | believe he only required 1:1 and this was
reflected in my Roster of Care (ROC) and the SIL request. She objected but | stated that
the behavioural assessment | had arranged, also confirmed the need for 1:1 and that’s
how | would be presenting the application. This is a common trend - Support
Coordinators are being directed by Vida to process SIL requests at a higher ratio of need
in order to be able to charge for higher supports, while never actually providing them.
These Support Coordinators are either unaware as they are inexperienced, or aware,
but not brave enough to speak up due to potential ramifications. What has happened in
the past appears that anyone who questions the legitimacy of procedures have been
sacked or have resigned under bizarre circumstances, and their reputations smeared
within the company by Vida. | don’t think any of them have actually made an official
complaint about suspected fraud prior to this current one by myself and my colleagues.

TO WHOM IT MAY CONCERN

iam writing to add comment to the above Case Number relating to suspected
fraudulent activity by The Hybrid Hive (The Hive) and parent company, Forward Focused
Group. My comment adds weight to those of my colleagues who work across various
sections of these companies, who have already emailed you with their suspicions.

| would ask that my name remain anonymous as | am still employed by Forward Focused
Group and fam concerned about the potential ramifications if my employers are aware |
have sent this email. | have chosen to remain at Forward Focused Group as | am
concerned about my participants and the impact that this fraudulent activity may have
on them if | leave.

t have worked at Forward Focused since 1° December 2021 and it wasn’t until February
2022 that | was alerted to fraudulent activity. One of my participants, Yvonne Harrison,
NDIS 431664181, DOB 27/10/65, was nearing the end of her plan, with approx. $40,000
remaining in her plan. Yvonne’s Plan Manager queried a number of invoices from The
Hive for support workers that were sent at around the same time, for large and
uncharacteristic amounts. She forwarded copies of these to me, and she was confused
because sorne of them were for thousands of dollars and they were dated from early
2021, even though Yvonne had not signed on with Forward Focused until October 2021.
| was suspicious that the invoices were raised in order to use up the funds that were
remaining in Yvonne’s plan. | approached the head of finance and queried the invoices
and he told me there must be an error and leave it to him. | overheard a phone call
where Vida Reid (Director) was explaining the “mistake” to Yvonne and was reassuring
her that she needn’t worry about dealing with the Pian Manager as it made her too
anxious. The invoices in question were then voided and not charged to Yvonne. Yvonne
was encouraged to change Plan Managers and the balance of her plan was transferred
accordingly. Shortly afterwards her new plan was approved, and the invoices were
raised again and amounts totaling over $30,000 for support workers were charged to
Yvonne’s old plan, again with service dates prior to when she started with Forward
Focused.

The Hive had never provided support workers for Yvonne until 15/03/22 and have never
provided a cleaner. Since 15/03/22, Yvonne has received 6 hours of support per week
and yet The Hive are invoicing regularly for 44 hours of support per month pius 8 hours
of cleaning.

Early in March 2022 | took a phone call from Johnathon Shapiera, NDIS 431201599, DOB
02/09/1956. He was querying why his plan funds seemed to be depleted so quickly. He
mentioned that he had used STA for respite recently so he was aware that cost had
come out of his funding but thought he should have more than what was showing on his
portal. As his Support Coordinator was on leave, | told him | would look into it for him. |
approached a member of the finance team and discovered that three different amounts
for STA had been invoiced in recent months. Once again, | referred this to the head of
finance and he said he would deal with it and get back to Johnathon. I’m not sure of the
outcome of this.

| recently assisted a new Support Coordinator to complete a separate Roster of Care for
two participants. In each case she had been instructed by the Director to complete
these showing 2:1 supports being required 24/7 when | know that one participant is
currently only receiving 1:1 support for a few hours a day and the other one currently
has no supports. | believe that the intention is to place these participants in SIL housing
with funding in place for high level supports and then provide no or very few supports as
is already happening with Andrew Hahn and Meghan Goodwin. (This situation has
already been highlighted to you by my colleagues.)

tam happy to provide you with any further information, if required. i again request that
you keep my identity anonymous.

Hi Janine,

T9.17

cleaning was provided 3 times a week, being Tuesday, Thursday and Saturdays for 3 hours each session
along with being billed for both travel time and km reimbursement. This equates to 9 hours of cleaning a
week for a small 1 bdrm place. Certainly, something that does not fit the parameters of being fair and
reasonable under NDIS practice standards. You will also note cleaning occurred for 3 hours on Christrnas
day (25/12/21) as well as Boxing Day (28/12/21) and New Year's Day (1/1/22), all public holidays.

The km reimbursement is 38.2 on each occasion. | am assuming then that your employee who
undertook the cleaning came to your offices first in Armadale and left from here to clean Suzie’s place as
it is 38.9km from Armadale to Northbridge. However, if your employee left from her home to Suzie’s
this cannot be charged, or the charge of km’s should vary if your employee was leaving a previous
clients place prior to heading to clean Suzie’s place. Where cleaning occurred on the 3 public holidays
mentioned above, did the employee leave from their home of residence to clean or from your offices, as
Forward Focused was closed over the Christmas break from 24 December through to the 4th of January,
as published on 22/12/21 on your organisations Facebook page. | am assuming due to your offices being
closed, they did not first come to the offices before heading onto Suzie’s place. The km reimbursement
should have varied on these days unless the cleaner was leaving from their home, in which case this
cannot be charged for.

managed, but was rejected. | am wondering did the cleaning continue after the invoice was rejected and
if so why was the decision made to continue?

PART 11. INTRODUCTIONARY NOTE AND OBJECTIVES

1.1 FORWARD FOCUSED GROUP Pty Ltd (ABN: 98 643 878 639) T/A FFG is

an approved service provider for National Disability Insurance Agency,

1.2 FFG aims to support people with disability (including psychosocial disability)

in all aspects of their support needs. It was identified by FFG that people
with disability often find it difficult to access suitable community
accommodation. FFG has taken an initiative by purchasing or leasing
houses to provide accommodation support, in a shared living arrangement,
for people with disability. The accommodation is provided on a fixed term
basis in line with support funding, as set out in the Schedules of this
agreement. The accommodation is linked to the support needs as set in
the Schedules.

2. PARTIES

2.1

2.2

2.3

This Accommodation Agreement is made between:

FFG WA (Provider) ABN: 98 643 878 639 of
2/210 Jull Street

Armadale WA 6112

Australia and

Participant Name: Su 2672 Nos: 4.3) tom 50F

who is a Participant in the National Disability Insurance Scheme (NDIS)
ONG oe eeeceeeeeseeeeeneees (Participant Representative).

This Accommodation Agreement will commence on 15th Nov 2021 for the
period of 3 months and NDIS plan dates:

3. CONDITION OF ACCESS TO Forward Focused Group (FFG) SHARED

COMMUNITY ACCOMMODATION

3.1 The Client and the representative agree that the accommodation is offered at

FFG's Disability Service’s Community Housing based on the following
conditions:

a) FFG's Disability Services will be the sole service provider to the
Client for the service provision set out in the Schedule of Support of
this agreement;

b) FFG's Disability Services is funded and continues to be funded by
National Disability Insurance Agency, Department of Communities-
Disability Services (WA) and Department of Heaith- Commonwealth
Continuity of Support (CoS).

c) FFG's Disability Services has the right to request the Client vacate
the premises on the day the support set out in the attached
Schedules is ceased or terminated or altered;

408

T9.17

=== PDF page 419 (T9.17) [OCR] ===
d) The Client and the representative agree that they must vacate the
premises on the day the support set out in the Schedules is ceased,
terminated or altered;

e) FFG’s Disability Services may not provide relocation support at the
end of the term of this agreement or at the termination of the
agreement.

4. TERM OF AGREEMENT
4.1 This Seay accommodation agreement is fixed for the period

Note: The start date for the agreement should not be a date prior to the
date on which the Client is entitled to enter into occupation of the
premises.

5. CLIENTIAL PREMISES

5.1 The Residential premises are described in Schedule 1. The Client has full
access to the designated room and access to and use of the Kitchen,
Family Room, Laundry, Bathroom/toilet, Garage, and Outdoor areas.

6. RENT

6.1 The rent is set out in Schedule 1 and is payable fortnightly in advance starting
17/11/2021 .

6.2 For a Client in receipt of new start allowance the rent may be negotiated and

FFG's Disability Services may offer a reduced rent. This reduced rent will
increase immediately when the Client cornmences receiving the full
disability support pension.

6.3 For a Client aged between 18 — 21 the rent may be negotiated and FFG's
Disability Services may offer a reduced rent. The reduced rent will increase
immediately at age 21 or when the client commences receiving the full
pension, whichever comes first.

6.4.

6.5 FFG's Disability Services may issue a notice of termination and, if the rent is
still not paid in full, FFG’s Disability Services may advise the Client to
vacate the premises without further notice.

6.6 The Client must not withhold rent because the Client is of the view that FFG's
Disability Services is in breach of the agreement.

6.7 FFG's Disability Services will provide a receipt to the Client on request unless
the rent is paid into an authorised bank or credit union account nominated
by FFG's Disability Services.

409

T9.17

=== PDF page 420 (T9.17) [OCR] ===
7. SECURITY DEPOSIT

7.1 A security Deposit may be requested to be paid by the Client on signing this
agreement. The Security Deposit is maintained in FFG’s Account.

7.2 FFG's Disability deserves the right to recover the unpaid rent from the Client's
Security Deposit.

8. RENT INCREASE

8.1 FFG's Disability Services may increase the rent in March and September each
year to match the published disability pension adjustments from the
(Australian Government) Department of Human Services.

9. WATER SERVICES AND WATER USAGE COSTS

9.1 The rent is not inclusive of water services and water usage. The Client is
required to pay water consumption costs.

10. ELECTRICITY, GAS AND OTHER UTILITIES

10.1 The rent is not inclusive of the cost of electricity and gas consumption. The
Client is required to pay the consumption of electricity and gas.

11. PETS
11.1 Pets are not permitted to be kept on the premises unless approved by the
Manager.

11.2 If itis agreed that a pet may be kept al the premises, then a Pet
Agreement must be signed in conjunction with the Accommodation
Agreement.

12. SUBLETTING

12.1 Clients are not permitted to sublet the whole or any part of the property. It
should not be expected this permission will readily be given.

13. RIGHT OF CLIENT TO AFFIX AND REMOVE FIXTURES

13.1. The Client must not affix any fixture or make any renovation, alteration or
addition to the premises.

13.2 The Client may only affix any fixture or make any renovation, alteration or
addition to the premises with the Regional Managers written permission.

14. PROPERTY CONDITION REPORTS

14.1. A report on the condition of the property may be offered to a Client upon
Client's request.

14.2 If the Client disagrees with any information contained in the property
condition report, the Client must note his or her disagreement on a copy of
the property condition report and return this to the Manager within 7 days

410

T9.17

=== PDF page 421 (T9.17) [OCR] ===
of receipt of the property condition report. If the Client does not give a copy
of the property condition report back to the Manager, the Client is taken to
accept the property condition report as a true and accurate description of
the condition of the premises.

15. RIGHT TO OCCUPY THE PREMISES

15.1 The Client has the right to exclusive occupation of the apartment (as
described the Schedule) in the premises and quiet enjoyment of the
Residential premises during the tenancy.

16. COPY OF AGREEMENT

16.1 FFG's Disability Services must give the Client/representative a copy of this
agreement when this agreement is signed by both the
Client/representative and FFG's Disability Services.

17. USE OF THE PREMISES BY CLIENT
17.1 The Client must:
a) use the premises as a place of residence; and
b} not use or allow the premises to be used for any illegal purpose; and

c) not cause or permit a nuisance; and not intentionally or negligently
cause or permit damage to the Residential premises; and

d) advise the property manager as soon as practicable, if any damage
occurs; and

e) keep the premises in a reasonable state of cleanliness; and

f) not cause or allow to be caused injury to the representative of FFG's
Disability Services, Co-Clients or any person lawfully on adjacent
premises; and not allow anyone who is lawfully at the premises to
breach the terms of this agreement.

17.2 The Client is responsible for the conduct or omission of any person lawfully
on the premises that results in a breach of the agreement.

19 SMOKING

19.1 The Client is not permitted to smoke inside the premises (house) under any
circumstances. FFG's Disability deserves the right to terminate the
tenancy, if the Client breach this clause.

19.2 Client may smoke outside the house

T9.17

=== PDF page 422 (T9.17) [OCR] ===
22.3 The Client will be responsible for the insurance of their own furniture,
electrical and white goods and personal effects.

22.4 FFG’s Disability Services is not liable for any damages or loss to Client’s
personal furniture, electrical and white goods and personal effects.

23, LOCKS AND SECURITY DEVICES

23.1 FFG’s Disability Services provide the Client a set of keys to their designated
room and ail common doors to the premises. FFG’s Disability Services
takes necessary means to ensure the premises are reasonably secure.

23.2 Any lock or security device at the premises must not be altered, removed or
duplicated by Clients or FFG’s Disability Services without the consent of
the other; and

23.3 FFG's Disability Services or the Client must not unreasonably withhold that
consent.

24. VISITORS

24.2 itis expected that visitors will not cause any inconvenience to other Clients
in a shared situation or peopie in the neighborhood.

24.3 FFG’s Disability Services has the right to request the visitors to leave the
premises, if they cause inconvenience to other Clients in a shared situation
or peopie in the neighborhood.

24.4 At no time shouid the Client allow any illegal activities to be carried out by
any visitor.

26. NEIGHBOURS

26.1 Clients are expected to respect the right of any person to live in the
neighborhood of surrounding their accommodation.

26.2 Any problems or disputes with neighbors should be reported to the manager
or a coordinator who will facilitate procedures to resolve the issue.

27. TERMINATING THE TENANCY

27.1 The accommodation is provided to the Client in a mutual agreeance FFG's
Disability Services and the Client, that FFG’s Disability Services will and
continues to provide ail the support described in the Schedule of Support
of this agreement.

27.2 If the Client chooses to cease support from FFG’s Disability Services for any
or all of the support as described in the Schedule of Support, the Client will
cease the right to occupy the premises for accommodation.

27.3 If the Client chooses to terminate care support as described in the Medium
term accommodation line item in the National disability insurance scheme

412

T9.17

=== PDF page 423 (T9.17) [OCR] ===
price guide,the Client must vacate the premises on the day the care support
ceases.

27.4 FFG's Disability Services deserve the right to terminate the accommodation

27.5

agreement and request the Client to vacate the premise on the day the
support needs set out in the Medium term accommodation line item in the
National disability insurance scheme price guide, ceased, terminated,
altered or amended either by Client or other stakeholders.

A Client may terminate the tenancy at any time by giving FFG’s
Disability Services at least 30 days’ notice in writing to the Manager of their
intention to do so.

27.6 FFG’s Disability Services may terminate the tenancy by providing 14 days’

notice, if the Client's care and support needs are above the requirement set
out in the Schedule of Support.

27.7 The Client agrees, when this agreement ends, to give vacant possession of

the premises to FFG’s Disability Services. Before giving vacant possession
to FFG’s Disability Services the Client must:

a) remove ail the Client's goods from the Residential premises; and

b) leave the Residential premises as closely as possible in the same
condition as at the commencement of the tenancy; and

c) return all keys, and other opening devices or similar devices,
provided by FFG's Disability Services.

d) The Client may be liable for losses incurred by FFG’s Disability
Services if the above requirements are not met.

e) FFG's Disability Services does not accept any responsibility for
unclaimed properties or personal effects that are left in the premises
after the end of the termination date.

28. ENDING THE FIXED-TERM AGREEMENT

28.1 This is a fixed-term agreement which will end as described in the Schedule;

or by agreement in writing between FFG's Disability Services and the
Client; or if either FFG's Disability Services or Client does not want to renew
the agreement, by giving written notice of termination. The notice must be
given to the other party at least 30 days prior to the date on which vacant
possession of the premises is to be delivered to FFG’s Disability Services.
The notice may be given at any time up until the end of the fixed term but
cannot take effect until the term ends.

29. OTHER GROUNDS FOR ENDING AGREEMENT

29.1

FFG's Disability Services may end this agreement:

a) on the sale of the Residential premises;

413

T9.17

=== PDF page 424 (T9.17) [OCR] ===
Db) if the property is no longer for lease (applicable to rental properties)

c) breach of this agreement by the Client, where the agreement is
frustrated e.g. where the premises are destroyed and hardship; or

d) when the funding as set out in the Schedule of this agreement
ceased, terminated or altered.

e) Client's care and support needs become above the requirement set
out in the Schedule.

30. CLIENT LIABILITY

30.1 FFG’s Disability Services will, when allocating tenancy, take into
consideration the ability of a Client to understand the law of liability. If the
Client has, or develops, behavioural issues that could impact on their ability
to remain in the tenancy, FFG’s Disability Services will negotiate with the
Client, their family, Guardian or advocates a suitable re-location.

31. CONFIDENTIALITY

31.1 Every employee and volunteer of FFG’s Disability Services is required to sign
a declaration of Confidentiality and Privacy prior to commencing their
employment or involvement with FFG's Disability Services.

32. CLIENTS RIGHTS AND RESPONSIBILITIES

32.1 FFG's Disability Services acknowledges the Clients’ right to:

a) fair and non-discriminatory treatment;
b) be treated with respect;

c) have access to safe and secure housing;

d) be consulted on housing needs and preferences;
e) the maintenance of confidentiality of personal information provided;
f) be consulted on substantial changes in the way the tenancy is managed;

g) security of tenure within the terms of the tenancy agreement and funding
guidelines;

h) complain and appeal against decisions made within FFG’s Disability
Services;

i) use of an advocate at any time;

j) have access to their own information heid by FFG’s Disability Services in
any form.

Assistive Technology (AT) items.

place by the end of the week.

YOU ARE OUR FOCUS

The NDIS and this Service Agreement
This Service Agreement is made for the purpose of providing support
services (Supports or Services) under the Participant’s National Disability
Insurance Scheme (NDIS) Plan.

The Parties agree that this Service Agreement is made in the context of
the NDIS, which is a scheme that aims to:

* Support the independence and social and economic participation of people with a disability;
and

+ Enable people with a disability to exercise their choice and control in pursuit if their goals,
and the planning and delivery of their supports.

The terms and conditions contained in this Service Agreement (as varied from time to time in
writing) will apply to any Services provided by FFG.

Glossary of Terms

FFG means the provider of Services under this agreement (otherwise known as we or us).
NDIA means the National Disability Insurance Agency.

Service Agreement means this agreement and any attached documents or schedules.

You means a person who is a receiver of Services or a person such as a family member or friend
who has authority to act on behalf of the service recipient under the National Disability Insurance
Scheme, as the context requires.

1. FFG’s responsibilities

We will use our best efforts to:

(a) Provide the Services described in clause 4 of this Service Agreement, and/or the attached
Schedule of Services, as varied from time to time;

(b) Plan with you for the provision of the Services as outlined in your NDIS plan;
(c) Consult with you about how Services will be provided and times for these Services;
(d) Review the provision of your Services quarterly;

(e) Communicate any changes, and discuss with you any barriers, to delivering any Supports
or Services as per the plan, including those outside of our control;

(f) Provide you with information about our procedure for the reporting and management of
complaints;

(g) Provide you with details of our Cancellation Procedure — See Point 6.

(h) Provide a minimum of 24 hours’ notice where possible, when we have to change a
scheduled appointment to provide the Services;

(i) Comply with all rules and regulations regarding the privacy of your personal information;
(j) Keep accurate records on the Services provided to you;

(k) We only work 9 am to 5 pm Monday to Friday. We will not respond to sms or calls after or
before these hours.

Page 2 of 8

487 Doc ID: 89a5c0559d3db480a7c27 143a313f4917f8743ae

T09.49 Attachment 41 - FFG $(48789709.1).pdf 2 8/08/2023 10:14:23 AM

=== PDF page 498 (T9.49) [OCR] ===
Document created: 1.11.2020 79.49
Updated: 17.12.2021 ey FOCUSED

YOU ARE OUR FOCUS

(I) We can only link you to supports that fall into the guidelines of the NDIS act 2013 of
reasonable and necessary criteria.

(m) Our job is not case management as we are support coordinators. We will only connect you
to the supports you ask us to and link you to them.

(n) We ask that you have realistic expectations and work with us to achieve a good outcome.

(0) Provide the Services in a manner consistent with all relevant laws, including the National
Disability insurance Scheme Act 2013, and the Australian Consumer Law; and (I) Provide
you with regular statements of the Services we deliver to you.

(p) We will not provide transport assistance, pick or drop you off to places as that is outside of
our role. We will only meet you where you have asked us to and then leave. Most of our
work will be done over the phone as we are here to connect you to supports. We are not
support workers.

(q) From time to time as required we may have to work with other organisations for our
complex case clients and as such we will also charge the costs incurred for sourcing
additional supports.

2. Your obligations

You agree that, during the duration of this Services Agreement, you will:
(a) Inform us of how you would like your Services to be provided by us to meet your needs;
(b) Always treat FFG Support Coordinators with courtesy and respect.

(c) Communicate with FFG Support Coordinator if you have any concerns about the Services
being provided in accordance with our procedure for the reporting of complaints.

(d) Comply with all of the terms of this Services Agreement; and

(e) Immediately inform FFG Support Coordinator if your NDIS plan is suspended or replaced
by a new NDIS plan or if you stop being a participant in the NDIS.

(f) Not ask us to provide supports outside of our role.

3. NDIS plan outcomes

Support type Outcome

Support Coordination To assist in the navigation of the plan, assist with service
providers and supports, improve social participation.

4. Services

YOU ARE OUR FOCUS

The Services to be delivered by FFG in this Services Agreement set out below and will be limited to
the type and level of the funding allocated to FFG in your NDIS plan.

FFG will not provide any Services or Supports that exceed the level of funding allocated to FFG in
your NDIS plan.

All fees or charges for Services or Supports under this Services Agreement will be in accordance
with the NDIS Price Guide. Capacity Building budget

Support Coordination is included in the Capacity Building budget. This is a fixed amount for
strengthening participants’ abilities to co-ordinate and implement supports in their plans and to
participate more fully in the community.

FFG Responsibilities subject to your funding
* Provide information and supports that may be available to you
+ Ensure that you have genuine choice and control of your NDIS plan
+ Support you in resolving service delivery issues
+ Regular management of your supports throughout your NDIS plan
* Develop and collating reports for reviews
5. Reports

FFG is required to provide report(s) to the NDIA 6 weeks before your plan is due for review. The
report will document how the Services provided by FFG have contributed to the achievement of

goals in your NDIS plan as well as provide recommendations to inform your future NDIS plan. 6.
Cancellations

(a) If:
i. a Service is less than 8 hours’ continuous duration; and
ii. the total price for the Service is less than $1,000.00

then you must provide at least 2 clear business days’ notice to cancel or reschedule that
Service.

(b) For any other Services, you must provide at least 5 clear business days’ notice to cancel or
reschedule that Service.

(c) If you do not provide the notice required under this clause, we may charge you up to 90% of
the charge for that cancelled or rescheduled Service.

7. Occupational Safety and Health

Page 4 of 8

489 Doc ID: 89a5c0559d3db480a7c27 143a313f4917f8743ae

T09.49 Attachment 41 - FFG S(48789709.1).pdf 4 8/08/2023 10:14:24 AM

YOU ARE OUR FOCUS

An Occupational Safety and Health Assessment Report of your premises may be completed and
provided by FFG. A copy will be kept in the FFG file at your residence if required.

An Individual Care Plan and Service Planner can be completed by FFG staff and kept on file at
your residence (if applicable).

8. Payment for the Services

The Services provided by FFG must be paid to FFG by:
(a) NDIA
(b) the Participant, if your NDIS plan is self-managed; or (c) a nominated plan
manager if your NDIS plan is managed.
[delete/cross out whichever is not applicable].
If you or your nominated plan manager are responsible for payment of the Services:
(a) FFG will issue fortnightly invoices for the Services; and
(b) Payment for the Services must be made within 14 days of the date of each invoice.

You may also request an activity statement at any time.
Where the Services are paid by the NDIA:

(a) FFG will issue weekly invoices to the NDIA.

(b) The pricing in your plan is inclusive of the time required for all activities related to the
Service including assessment, planning and preparation, liaison, report writing, training,
direct contact (e.g. face to face, phone, teleconference, skype, email) and indicate the
potential hours for assessment, planning and intervention. For example, NDIS will pay for a
physiotherapist to write a report to NDIA, or for the time taken to liaise with other service
providers/health professional regarding your care/services or research on your behalf.

(c) If the NDIA change the price payable to service providers for the Services:
a. your plan will be automatically adjusted to account for the increase in prices; and

b. we may increase the prices we charge to you in accordance with any corresponding
increase in the prices payable to service providers by the NDIA.

(d) There can be a delay in between the expiry of this plan and the completion of a new service
agreement for your renewed plan. FFG will continue to provide Services in accordance with
this Service Agreement after the expiry date of your current plan to ensure continuity. If you
decide to use another service provider for your renewed plan, please notify FFG of this
immediately. You will need to ensure that FFG receive payment for the Services during this
period, in accordance with this Service Agreement.

(e) If you receive notification that the NDIA has, for whatever reason, withdrawn funding for the
Services, you must notify FFG within 7 days. If you do not notify FFG within 7 days, you
may be personally be liable for payment of any Services provided by FFG.

9. Provider Travel

We will charge you for travel in accordance with the NDIS Price Guide, as amended from time to
time.

10. Changes to this Service Agreement

If changes to the Services, or their delivery, are required, FFG and the Participant agree to discuss
and review this Service Agreement.

From time to time, FFG can make alterations to this Service Agreement due to changes in the
policy and procedure of the NDIA.

FFG and the Participant agree that any changes to this Service Agreement will be in writing.

11. Ending this Service Agreement

Either Party may terminate this Service Agreement by giving the other Party one month’s notice in
writing.

12. Feedback, Complaints and Disputes

Feedback, complaints and disputes The FFG welcomes feedback.
How to raise your concerns and complaints:

1. Tell your support staff or your case manager. This is often the quickest way to address and
resolve your concerns.

2. You can also raise your concerns and complaints with the Manager of the service.

3. FGG’s website https://forwardfocusedgroup.com.au also has a Feedback Form you can
complete. This form will be sent to the FFG’s Manager Policy, Planning & Development.

If you are not satisfied or do not want to talk to this person, you can contact the National Disability
Insurance Agency by calling 1800 800 110, visiting one of their offices in person, or visiting the
website ndis.gov.au for further information.

13. Goods and Services Tax (GST)

All prices are GST inclusive (if applicable).
For the purposes of GST legislation, you agree that:

The Services described in this Service Agreement are reasonable and necessary supports
specified in the statement of supports in the Participant's NDIS plan currently in effect under
section 37 of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013;

The Participant’s NDIS plan is expected to remain in effect during the period the Services are
provided; and

The Participant/Participant’s representative will immediately notify the FFG if the Participant’s
NDIS Plan is replaced or the Participant stops being a participant in the NDIS.

This statement made by me accurately sets out the evidence that | would be

prepared, if necessary, to give in court as a witness.

My full name is Cassandra Lee Burke. | prefer to be called Cassie. | am 30 years of
age. My residential address is known to the National Disability Insurance Agency
(NDIA).

| am a support Coordinator and work for Access Disability Services. | have been

employed at Access since October 2022.

Prior to that | worked at Forward Focus Group (FFG). | started work there in June
or July of 2022 and | was employed as a Support Coordinator.

| know Vida REID. | first met Vida on my first day at FFG. My first impression of
Vida was that she was very friendly, she was kind, caring, friendly and | can

remember thinking ‘what a cool boss’. She also put off a really successful vibe.

Initially, | was a receptionist but after three weeks | was promoted to Support

Coordinator.

The next day we all came back in, and our computers were waiting on the reception

desk. We just grabbed them and went back to work like nothing had happened.
Throughout my time as a support coordinator, | managed about 18 participants.

With email messages, it was common for Vida to change what we had written
before sending the email. For example, after Vida’s arrest, she instructed all staff
who had come back to the office to email our local member of parliament to say that
the allegations made against the company we work for are seriously affecting us

and our jobs, that we had families, and we were scared we would lose our jobs.

Vida instructed all staff to prepare a draft and send it to her. She would alter things

in the email and then send it back with instructions to send it.

| remember that | didn’t even want to write the email, and | remember that Vida
changed my email with some better words to make it read more like a sob story.

Then she sent it back to me and told me to send it.

| would say that no one could be confident that an email received from an FFG
employee had been written by the person who's signature block appeared at the

bottom of it. All emails were subject to change by Vida.

| remember on 25 October 2022, | remember Paul Ugwunali came into the FFG
office and met with Vida. Vida then told Ashleigh to go into the office with Paul and
they had a meeting. Ashleigh came out and Vida asked me to go in.

Vida introduced me to Paul and told me that he had a disability support company
and that she was very sorry, but things were so unstable that she couldn't offer me
work anymore, but Paul was willing to put me on full time if | signed the contract that

day and | could take my clients with me.
Vida then left and | signed the contract with Paul.

Paul then said, “Can you please get your client information?” ( \\ /

YOU ARE OUR FOCUS

The NDIS and this Service Agreement
This Service Agreement is made for the purpose of providing support
services (Supports or Services) under the Participant’s National Disability
Insurance Scheme (NDIS) Plan.

The Parties agree that this Service Agreement is made in the context of
the NDIS, which is a scheme that aims to:

* Support the independence and social and economic participation of people with a disability;
and

+ Enable people with a disability to exercise their choice and control in pursuit if their goals,
and the planning and delivery of their supports.

The terms and conditions contained in this Service Agreement (as varied from time to time in
writing) will apply to any Services provided by FFG.

Glossary of Terms

FFG means the provider of Services under this agreement (otherwise known as we or us).
NDIA means the National Disability Insurance Agency.

Service Agreement means this agreement and any attached documents or schedules.

You means a person who is a receiver of Services or a person such as a family member or friend
who has authority to act on behalf of the service recipient under the National Disability Insurance
Scheme, as the context requires.

1. FFG’s responsibilities

We will use our best efforts to:

(a) Provide the Services described in clause 4 of this Service Agreement, and/or the attached
Schedule of Services, as varied from time to time;

(b) Plan with you for the provision of the Services as outlined in your NDIS plan;
(c) Consult with you about how Services will be provided and times for these Services;
(d) Review the provision of your Services quarterly;

(e) Communicate any changes, and discuss with you any barriers, to delivering any Supports
or Services as per the plan, including those outside of our control;

(f) Provide you with information about our procedure for the reporting and management of
complaints;

(g) Provide you with details of our Cancellation Procedure — See Point 6.

(h) Provide a minimum of 24 hours’ notice where possible, when we have to change a
scheduled appointment to provide the Services;

(i) Comply with all rules and regulations regarding the privacy of your personal information;
(j) Keep accurate records on the Services provided to you;

(k) We only work 9 am to 5 pm Monday to Friday. We will not respond to sms or calls after or
before these hours.

Page 2 of 8

1 449 Doc ID: 89a5c0559d3db480a7c27 143a313f4917f8743ae

123. PDMS (EC22-0020(48782019.1).pdf 2 7/08/2023 3:04:43 PM

=== PDF page 1484 (T93) [OCR] ===

Updated: 17.12.2021 wz FOCUSED

YOU ARE OUR FOCUS

(I) We can only link you to supports that fall into the guidelines of the NDIS act 2013 of
reasonable and necessary criteria.

(m) Our job is not case management as we are support coordinators. We will only connect you
to the supports you ask us to and link you to them.

(n) We ask that you have realistic expectations and work with us to achieve a good outcome.

(0) Provide the Services in a manner consistent with all relevant laws, including the National
Disability insurance Scheme Act 2013, and the Australian Consumer Law; and (I) Provide
you with regular statements of the Services we deliver to you.

(p) We will not provide transport assistance, pick or drop you off to places as that is outside of
our role. We will only meet you where you have asked us to and then leave. Most of our
work will be done over the phone as we are here to connect you to supports. We are not
support workers.

(q) From time to time as required we may have to work with other organisations for our
complex case clients and as such we will also charge the costs incurred for sourcing
additional supports.

2. Your obligations

You agree that, during the duration of this Services Agreement, you will:
(a) Inform us of how you would like your Services to be provided by us to meet your needs;
(b) Always treat FFG Support Coordinators with courtesy and respect.

(c) Communicate with FFG Support Coordinator if you have any concerns about the Services
being provided in accordance with our procedure for the reporting of complaints.

(d) Comply with all of the terms of this Services Agreement; and

(e) Immediately inform FFG Support Coordinator if your NDIS plan is suspended or replaced
by a new NDIS plan or if you stop being a participant in the NDIS.

(f) Not ask us to provide supports outside of our role.

3. NDIS plan outcomes

Support type Outcome

Support Coordination To assist in the navigation of the plan, assist with service
providers and supports, improve social participation.

4. Services

The Services to be delivered by FFG in this Services Agreement set out below and will be limited to
the type and level of the funding allocated to FFG in your NDIS plan.

FFG will not provide any Services or Supports that exceed the level of funding allocated to FFG in
your NDIS plan.

All fees or charges for Services or Supports under this Services Agreement will be in accordance
with the NDIS Price Guide. Capacity Building budget

Support Coordination is included in the Capacity Building budget. This is a fixed amount for
strengthening participants’ abilities to co-ordinate and implement supports in their plans and to
participate more fully in the community.

FFG Responsibilities subject to your funding
* Provide information and supports that may be available to you
+ Ensure that you have genuine choice and control of your NDIS plan
+ Support you in resolving service delivery issues
+ Regular management of your supports throughout your NDIS plan
* Develop and collating reports for reviews
5. Reports

FFG is required to provide report(s) to the NDIA 6 weeks before your plan is due for review. The
report will document how the Services provided by FFG have contributed to the achievement of

goals in your NDIS plan as well as provide recommendations to inform your future NDIS plan. 6.
Cancellations

(a) If:
i. a Service is less than 8 hours’ continuous duration; and
ii. the total price for the Service is less than $1,000.00

then you must provide at least 2 clear business days’ notice to cancel or reschedule that
Service.

(b) For any other Services, you must provide at least 5 clear business days’ notice to cancel or
reschedule that Service.

(c) If you do not provide the notice required under this clause, we may charge you up to 90% of
the charge for that cancelled or rescheduled Service.

7. Occupational Safety and Health

An Occupational Safety and Health Assessment Report of your premises may be completed and
provided by FFG. A copy will be kept in the FFG file at your residence if required.

An Individual Care Plan and Service Planner can be completed by FFG staff and kept on file at
your residence (if applicable).

8. Payment for the Services

The Services provided by FFG must be paid to FFG by:
(a) NDIA
(b) the Participant, if your NDIS plan is self-managed; or (c) a nominated plan
manager if your NDIS plan is managed.
[delete/cross out whichever is not applicable].
If you or your nominated plan manager are responsible for payment of the Services:
(a) FFG will issue fortnightly invoices for the Services; and
(b) Payment for the Services must be made within 14 days of the date of each invoice.

You may also request an activity statement at any time.
Where the Services are paid by the NDIA:

(a) FFG will issue weekly invoices to the NDIA.

(b) The pricing in your plan is inclusive of the time required for all activities related to the
Service including assessment, planning and preparation, liaison, report writing, training,
direct contact (e.g. face to face, phone, teleconference, skype, email) and indicate the
potential hours for assessment, planning and intervention. For example, NDIS will pay for a
physiotherapist to write a report to NDIA, or for the time taken to liaise with other service
providers/health professional regarding your care/services or research on your behalf.

(c) If the NDIA change the price payable to service providers for the Services:
a. your plan will be automatically adjusted to account for the increase in prices; and

b. we may increase the prices we charge to you in accordance with any corresponding
increase in the prices payable to service providers by the NDIA.

(d) There can be a delay in between the expiry of this plan and the completion of a new service
agreement for your renewed plan. FFG will continue to provide Services in accordance with
this Service Agreement after the expiry date of your current plan to ensure continuity. If you
decide to use another service provider for your renewed plan, please notify FFG of this
immediately. You will need to ensure that FFG receive payment for the Services during this
period, in accordance with this Service Agreement.

(e) If you receive notification that the NDIA has, for whatever reason, withdrawn funding for the
Services, you must notify FFG within 7 days. If you do not notify FFG within 7 days, you
may be personally be liable for payment of any Services provided by FFG.

9. Provider Travel

We will charge you for travel in accordance with the NDIS Price Guide, as amended from time to

10. Changes to this Service Agreement

If changes to the Services, or their delivery, are required, FFG and the Participant agree to discuss
and review this Service Agreement.

From time to time, FFG can make alterations to this Service Agreement due to changes in the
policy and procedure of the NDIA.

FFG and the Participant agree that any changes to this Service Agreement will be in writing.

11. Ending this Service Agreement

Either Party may terminate this Service Agreement by giving the other Party one month’s notice in
writing.

12. Feedback, Complaints and Disputes

Feedback, complaints and disputes The FFG welcomes feedback.
How to raise your concerns and complaints:

1. Tell your support staff or your case manager. This is often the quickest way to address and
resolve your concerns.

2. You can also raise your concerns and complaints with the Manager of the service.

3. FGG’s website https://forwardfocusedgroup.com.au also has a Feedback Form you can
complete. This form will be sent to the FFG’s Manager Policy, Planning & Development.

If you are not satisfied or do not want to talk to this person, you can contact the National Disability
Insurance Agency by calling 1800 800 110, visiting one of their offices in person, or visiting the
website ndis.gov.au for further information.

13. Goods and Services Tax (GST)

All prices are GST inclusive (if applicable).
For the purposes of GST legislation, you agree that:

The Services described in this Service Agreement are reasonable and necessary supports
specified in the statement of supports in the Participant's NDIS plan currently in effect under
section 37 of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013;

The Participant’s NDIS plan is expected to remain in effect during the period the Services are
provided; and

The Participant/Participant’s representative will immediately notify the FFG if the Participant’s
NDIS Plan is replaced or the Participant stops being a participant in the NDIS.

1: This statement made by me accurately sets out the evidence that | would be

prepared, if necessary, to give in court as a witness.

2. My full name is Sarah-Jane Hammond and | am 32 years old.

3. In March 2021, | was diagnosed with Autism by Dr Ritu Campbell, who encouraged
me to take the diagnosis report of hers to the National Disability Insurance Scheme
(NDIS). | then filled out the Access Request Form (ARF) after contacting the NDIS
and went into the Rockingham NDIS branch and submitted my paperwork in

person.

4. In April 2021, | was accepted as a participant of the NDIS and was given my NDIS
number 431685148. My first plan was from 22 April 2021 to 27 July 2022 which was
to the value of $61,325.91. My current NDIS plan is from 11 October 2022 to 10
October 2024 which is to the value of $36,895.60.

5: My understanding of the NDIS is to provide funding to pay for services in assisting
and supporting me with everyday living. The NDIS is there to cover the Gaps that
Medicare and other services cannot assist me with, | understand that it is not for

daily living expenses.

not remember ever receiving a copy of that Service Agreement. | do remember

we discussed in our meeting, as | only wanted a cleaner and a small amount of yard

The NDIS and this Service Agreement
This Service Agreement is made for the purpose of providing support
services (Supports or Services) under the Participant’s National Disability
Insurance Scheme (NDIS) Plan.

The Parties agree that this Service Agreement is made in the context of
the NDIS, which is a scheme that aims to:

* Support the independence and social and economic participation of people with a disability;
and

+ Enable people with a disability to exercise their choice and control in pursuit if their goals,
and the planning and delivery of their supports.

The terms and conditions contained in this Service Agreement (as varied from time to time in
writing) will apply to any Services provided by FFG.

Glossary of Terms

FFG means the provider of Services under this agreement (otherwise known as we or us).
NDIA means the National Disability Insurance Agency.

Service Agreement means this agreement and any attached documents or schedules.

You means a person who is a receiver of Services or a person such as a family member or friend
who has authority to act on behalf of the service recipient under the National Disability Insurance
Scheme, as the context requires.

1. FFG’s responsibilities

We will use our best efforts to:

(a) Provide the Services described in clause 4 of this Service Agreement, and/or the attached
Schedule of Services, as varied from time to time;

(b) Plan with you for the provision of the Services as outlined in your NDIS plan;
(c) Consult with you about how Services will be provided and times for these Services;
(d) Review the provision of your Services quarterly;

(e) Communicate any changes, and discuss with you any barriers, to delivering any Supports
or Services as per the plan, including those outside of our control;

(f) Provide you with information about our procedure for the reporting and management of
complaints;

(g) Provide you with details of our Cancellation Procedure — See Point 6.

(h) Provide a minimum of 24 hours’ notice where possible, when we have to change a
scheduled appointment to provide the Services;

(i) Comply with all rules and regulations regarding the privacy of your personal information;
(j) Keep accurate records on the Services provided to you;

(k) We only work 9 am to 5 pm Monday to Friday. We will not respond to sms or calls after or
before these hours.

YOU ARE OUR FOCUS

(I) We can only link you to supports that fall into the guidelines of the NDIS act 2013 of
reasonable and necessary criteria.

(m) Our job is not case management as we are support coordinators. We will only connect you
to the supports you ask us to and link you to them.

(n) We ask that you have realistic expectations and work with us to achieve a good outcome.

(0) Provide the Services in a manner consistent with all relevant laws, including the National
Disability insurance Scheme Act 2013, and the Australian Consumer Law; and (I) Provide
you with regular statements of the Services we deliver to you.

(p) We will not provide transport assistance, pick or drop you off to places as that is outside of
our role. We will only meet you where you have asked us to and then leave. Most of our
work will be done over the phone as we are here to connect you to supports. We are not
support workers.

(q) From time to time as required we may have to work with other organisations for our
complex case clients and as such we will also charge the costs incurred for sourcing
additional supports.

2. Your obligations

You agree that, during the duration of this Services Agreement, you will:
(a) Inform us of how you would like your Services to be provided by us to meet your needs;
(b) Always treat FFG Support Coordinators with courtesy and respect.

(c) Communicate with FFG Support Coordinator if you have any concerns about the Services
being provided in accordance with our procedure for the reporting of complaints.

(d) Comply with all of the terms of this Services Agreement; and

(e) Immediately inform FFG Support Coordinator if your NDIS plan is suspended or replaced
by a new NDIS plan or if you stop being a participant in the NDIS.

(f) Not ask us to provide supports outside of our role.

3. NDIS plan outcomes

Support type Outcome

Assistance with Daily Life 01_134_0117_8 1 - Self-Management Capacity
Building (Support Coordination)

4. Services

The Services to be delivered by FFG in this Services Agreement set out below and will be limited to
the type and level of the funding allocated to FFG in your NDIS plan.

FFG will not provide any Services or Supports that exceed the level of funding allocated to FFG in
your NDIS plan.

All fees or charges for Services or Supports under this Services Agreement will be in accordance
with the NDIS Price Guide. Capacity Building budget

Support Coordination is included in the Capacity Building budget. This is a fixed amount for
strengthening participants’ abilities to co-ordinate and implement supports in their plans and to
participate more fully in the community.

Support type | Cost Support delivered When/Where/How
Assistance with 100 Hours of Assistance with Daily Life
Daily Life $6,509.00

FFG Responsibilities subject to your funding
* Provide information and supports that may be available to you
* Ensure that you have genuine choice and control of your NDIS plan
* Support you in resolving service delivery issues
+ Regular management of your supports throughout your NDIS plan

* Develop and collating reports for reviews
5. Reports

FFG is required to provide report(s) to the NDIA 6 weeks before your plan is due for review. The
report will document how the Services provided by FFG have contributed to the achievement of

goals in your NDIS plan as well as provide recommendations to inform your future NDIS plan. 6.
Cancellations

(a) If:
i. a Service is less than 8 hours’ continuous duration; and
ii. the total price for the Service is less than $1,000.00

then you must provide at least 2 clear business days’ notice to cancel or reschedule that
Service.

(b) For any other Services, you must provide at least 5 clear business days’ notice to cancel or
reschedule that Service.

(c) If you do not provide the notice required under this clause, we may charge you up to 90% of
the charge for that cancelled or rescheduled Service.

7. Occupational Safety and Health

An Occupational Safety and Health Assessment Report of your premises may be completed and
provided by FFG. A copy will be kept in the FFG file at your residence if required.

An Individual Care Plan and Service Planner can be completed by FFG staff and kept on file at
your residence (if applicable).

8. Payment for the Services

The Services provided by FFG must be paid to FFG by:
(a) NDIA
(b) the Participant, if your NDIS plan is self-managed; or (c) a nominated plan
manager if your NDIS plan is managed.
[delete/cross out whichever is not applicable].
If you or your nominated plan manager are responsible for payment of the Services:
(a) FFG will issue fortnightly invoices for the Services; and
(b) Payment for the Services must be made within 14 days of the date of each invoice.

You may also request an activity statement at any time.
Where the Services are paid by the NDIA:
(a) FFG will issue weekly invoices to the NDIA.

(b) The pricing in your plan is inclusive of the time required for all activities related to the
Service including assessment, planning and preparation, liaison, report writing, training,
direct contact (e.g. face to face, phone, teleconference, skype, email) and indicate the
potential hours for assessment, planning and intervention. For example, NDIS will pay for a
physiotherapist to write a report to NDIA, or for the time taken to liaise with other service
providers/health professional regarding your care/services or research on your behalf.

(c) If the NDIA change the price payable to service providers for the Services:
a. your plan will be automatically adjusted to account for the increase in prices; and

b. we may increase the prices we charge to you in accordance with any corresponding
increase in the prices payable to service providers by the NDIA.

(d) There can be a delay in between the expiry of this plan and the completion of a new service
agreement for your renewed plan. FFG will continue to provide Services in accordance with
this Service Agreement after the expiry date of your current plan to ensure continuity. If you
decide to use another service provider for your renewed plan, please notify FFG of this
immediately. You will need to ensure that FFG receive payment for the Services during this
period, in accordance with this Service Agreement.

(e) If you receive notification that the NDIA has, for whatever reason, withdrawn funding for the
Services, you must notify FFG within 7 days. If you do not notify FFG within 7 days, you
may be personally be liable for payment of any Services provided by FFG.

We will charge you for travel in accordance with the NDIS Price Guide, as amended from time to
time.

10. Changes to this Service Agreement

If changes to the Services, or their delivery, are required, FFG and the Participant agree to discuss
and review this Service Agreement.

From time to time, FFG can make alterations to this Service Agreement due to changes in the
policy and procedure of the NDIA.

FFG and the Participant agree that any changes to this Service Agreement will be in writing.

11. Ending this Service Agreement

Either Party may terminate this Service Agreement by giving the other Party one month’s notice in
writing.

12. Feedback, Complaints and Disputes

Feedback, complaints and disputes The FFG welcomes feedback.
How to raise your concerns and complaints:

1. Tell your support staff or your case manager. This is often the quickest way to address and
resolve your concerns.

2. You can also raise your concerns and complaints with the Manager of the service.

3. FGG’s website https://forwardfocusedgroup.com.au also has a Feedback Form you can
complete. This form will be sent to the FFG’s Manager Policy, Planning & Development.

If you are not satisfied or do not want to talk to this person, you can contact the National Disability
Insurance Agency by calling 1800 800 110, visiting one of their offices in person, or visiting the
website ndis.gov.au for further information.

13. Goods and Services Tax (GST)

All prices are GST inclusive (if applicable).
For the purposes of GST legislation, you agree that:

The Services described in this Service Agreement are reasonable and necessary supports
specified in the statement of supports in the Participant's NDIS plan currently in effect under
section 37 of the National Disability Insurance Scheme Act 2013;

The Participant's NDIS plan is expected to remain in effect during the period the Services are
provided; and

The Participant/Participant’s representative will immediately notify the FFG if the Participant's
NDIS Plan is replaced or the Participant stops being a participant in the NDIS.
